# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 515–544

## Page 570 — IIIF Canvas #515

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0515.JP2` (5682 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0515.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0515.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

A two-column verso page (p. 570, pencil foliation 255) continuing fistula treatments with digestive salves, turpentine-balsam, and a watercolor/ink-wash illustration of a surgical probe/spatula, followed by recipes for wax/precipitate cones ('schöll weigel'). A rubricated section 'Von den Glid. Waser.' (On Synovial Fluid / Joint-Water) begins at the bottom of the left column and continues on the right column with pain-stilling salves and cataplasms.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Surgical probe or spatula** (`#xywh=400,400,2141,1000`): Horizontal ink-wash and pale ochre watercolor drawing in the middle of the left column depicting an elongated, flat surgical instrument (probe/spatula) with a rounded blunt tip on the left and a flared, trefoil-shaped handle on the right.

## Page 571 — IIIF Canvas #516

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0516.JP2` (6063 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0516.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0516.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

A two-column recto page (p. 571) continuing the treatment of joint-water ('glid waser') with plasters (Emplastrum Basiliconis, diasulphuris, diapalma, diachylon), a cataplasm to strengthen the limb and disperse swelling, a wound powder for internal and external wounds and nerve injuries, and several short recipes ('Ein anders fürs glid waser', 'Ein Anders') using pigeon dung, egg white, saffron, alum, and burnt leather.

## Page 572 — IIIF Canvas #517

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0517.JP2` (5742 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0517.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0517.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Full-height watercolor of a wounded man ('Wundmann') in the left column showing weapons, lesions, ulcers, a dog, and a snake/cord at his ankle; right column begins the section on wounds and dangerous injuries, specifically hot and cold gangrene (Gangraena and Sphacelus).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Wundmann (Wound Man) illustration** (`#xywh=400,400,2171,1000`): Full-height watercolor in the left column depicting a standing nude man wearing a yellow-ochre loincloth in a grassy landscape with distant trees and faint blue-grey clouds above. His body exhibits multiple wounds, red pustules, ulcers, and weapons: a curved sabre/scimitar piercing the crown of his head, a yellow club/cudgel striking the left side of his head, a red swelling on the right neck, a dagger in his left chest, a large red ulcer on his left upper arm, a blade through his left forearm, a sword piercing his left thigh, red/purple sores on his knees and shins, a grey dog sitting on the grass to the right, and a snake or cord coiled around his right ankle.

## Page 573 — IIIF Canvas #518

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0518.JP2` (6117 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0518.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0518.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of the treatise on hot and cold gangrene (Gangraena and Sphacelus), distinguishing their clinical signs, the role of the vital spirits (Spiritus Vitales) and radical moisture (humidum radicale), and the four humoral causes (blood, bile, phlegm, melancholy).

## Page 575 — IIIF Canvas #520

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0520.JP2` (6117 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0520.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0520.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of a treatise on inflammation and swelling, followed by a rubricated section on the cure and healing of hot gangrene ('heisen Brandts') arising from superfluous moisture, heat, or dryness, including dietary/surgical advice and recipes for defensive ointments, bloodletting/purging boluses, washes, and cataplasms.

### Left Column

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0521.JP2` (5686 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0521.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0521.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of gangrene remedies, followed by a rubricated section on causes from heat and dryness in erysipelas ('Rotlauff oder Schön'), and a rubricated section on arresting frostbite ('Erfrohren Auffhalten') with recipes for washes, salves, and cataplasms.

Continuation of frostbite cataplasm, followed by a rubricated section on gangrene caused by excessive dryness ('Ursachen auß Zů großen Drückne'), detailing dietary regimen, moistening baths, salves, plasters, surgical debridement, and a celery-juice salve ('Sälbel auß Eppich').

## Page 578 — IIIF Canvas #523

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0523.JP2` (5686 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0523.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0523.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 578 (fol. 259r) contains recipes for earthworm oil (Regenwürmöll), a treatise on the poisonous properties of gangrene/inflammation with sweating draughts using theriac, mithridate, bezoar, dittany, and angelica, and instructions for scarification, leeches, and a warm decoction.

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0524.JP2` (6046 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0524.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0524.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0525.JP2` (5649 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0525.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0525.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 580 (fol. 260r) presents remedies for hot and cold gangrene, a defensive ointment, a secret arcanum plaster for cold gangrene highlighted with a rubricated 'NB:' note, and a mercurial cleansing water (aqua fortis with quicksilver, mercurius dulcis, sugar of lead, and arcana corollina).

## Page 579 — IIIF Canvas #526

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0526.JP2` (6046 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0526.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0526.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Surgical instructions and recipes for treating gangrene (der heiße und kalte Brand), including wound debridement of the eschar, application of Unguentum Aegyptiacum and cataplasms, internal theriac/mithridate with blessed thistle water, cauterization or excision, and a rubricated red-ink salve and cataplasms for hot and cold gangrene.

## Page 580 — IIIF Canvas #527

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0527.JP2` (5687 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0527.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0527.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of remedies for cancer (Krebs), including Emplastrum de ranis cum mercurio, Terra Vitrioli dulcis, treatments for open and putrid cancer using burnt/washed lead, tobacco soot, sublimate precipitation, toad oil, Venetian soap, an arsenic/alum crucible preparation, and a rubricated wash for cancer.

## Page 585 — IIIF Canvas #528

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0528.JP2` (6071 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0528.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0528.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Treatise section 'Von dem Krebs' (Of Cancer), accompanied by a watercolor illustration of a reclining woman with a red crab clinging to her left breast (numbered 435.). Describes the onset, progression, pain, and malignancy of breast cancer, followed by internal antidotes and blood-cleansing remedies (diaphoretic powders, Bezoardicum Joviale et Martiale, Sal Saturni, viper powders, herbal distillates, and volatile spirits).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**435.** (`#xywh=400,400,2335,1000`): Watercolor illustration in the upper-left column showing a female patient reclining on a blue-and-white striped pillow, wearing a red cap and a grey garment open at the chest, with a bright red crab/lobster clinging to her left breast to symbolize breast cancer ('Krebs'). Framed below by a gold-and-brown band bearing the number '435.'.

Recto folio (original p. 584, pencil foliation 262) containing remedies for concealed and ulcerated cancer (Krebs), including a lead-opodeldoch plaster, a tobacco-bryony balsam, an astringent/caustic wash, a palliative lead-arsenic plaster, and a soothing salve with Venetian theriac and river crabs.

## Page 585 — IIIF Canvas #532

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0532.JP2` (6117 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0532.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0532.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 585 (verso) contains a recipe for a cleansing and healing salve for cancerous pain (using nightshade juice, crab juice, litharge, ceruse, rose water, myrtle oil, lily oil, tutty, precipitated mercury, and calamine), followed by a rubricated section on general and easier remedies for cancer, discussing the difficulty of curing cancer, the role of melancholic humor, and the three names of cancer (Noli me tangere, Lupus, and Krebs).

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0533.JP2` (5679 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0533.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0533.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 588 (recto, pencil foliation 264) begins with a faded rubricated heading, a styptic powder recipe (tragacanth, gum arabic, comfrey root, carpenter's glue), and then presents a rubricated account of an Italian ('Welscher') surgeon known as the 'Krebs Doctor' who cured cancers across Germany using a caustic arsenic-soot-arum powder, followed by protective defensive plasters and soothing ointments (frankincense, mummy, mastic, myrrh, aloes, sarcocolla, opopanax, litharge).

## Page 587 — IIIF Canvas #534

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0534.JP2` (6042 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0534.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0534.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 587 (verso) features a striking watercolor illustration of a woman undergoing surgical excision of a breast cancer (with a knife and crossed needles/ligatures), followed by a rubricated surgical procedure ('Wie der Krebs an der Brust Soll geschnitten werden') detailing how to tie four threads through the breast, pull it forward, excise the tumor, cauterize bleeding vessels with a hot iron, and dress the wound. The right column concludes a preceding note and displays a rubricated heading and full-length watercolor illustrations of surgical instruments (cautery irons, a large excision knife, and surgical needles/probes).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Surgical excision of breast cancer** (`#xywh=400,400,2321,1000`): Watercolor and ink bust illustration of a bare-chested woman wearing a blue-grey headscarf/coif, showing a large ulcerated breast tumor transfixed by a horizontal surgical knife and two crossed needles/pins with red ligature threads hanging downward, set above an olive-green painted border.

**Instrumenta Welche Zu dieser operation gebraucht werden.** (`#xywh=400,400,2321,1000`): Full-column watercolor and ink illustration of surgical instruments used for breast cancer excision: two long-handled cautery irons with turned brown wooden handles (one with a rectangular flat plate head and one with a round disc head), a large broad-bladed surgical knife with a carved wooden handle, and two slender surgical needles/probes on the far right.

## Page 592 — IIIF Canvas #537

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0537.JP2` (5704 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0537.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0537.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 592 (recto, pencil foliation 266) concludes the discussion of rabid animal bites (dogs, cats, snakes, scorpions) and blistering plasters/cupping treatments, followed by recipes for 'Der Hunde. Rosse. vnd Stieren biß' and 'Wan dich ein adter gebissen' (adder bite), a framed watercolor illustration of a person afflicted with pustules/scabies, and the opening of a chapter 'Von den Krezen' (On Scabies).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Von den Krezen** (`#xywh=400,400,2152,1000`): Framed watercolor illustration (blue and black ink border) showing a half-length profile of a fair-haired person in a reddish-brown tunic behind a horizontal bar, raising their right arm; their face, neck, and arms are covered in dark reddish-brown spots and pustules illustrating scabies ('Krezen').

## Page 593 — IIIF Canvas #538

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0538.JP2` (6030 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0538.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0538.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Page 593 (recto) of Wellcome MS.990 contains purgative recipes, decoctions, and bath and ointment remedies for scabies/itching (Krozen), including a watercolor illustration of a sulfur-soaked shirt worn for 5-6 days to cure itching.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Medicated Shirt for Curing Itching (Scabies)** (`#xywh=400,400,2315,1000`): Watercolor illustration in the right column depicting a knee-length, blue-and-white vertically striped shirt or tunic with short sleeves, standing on a small patch of green ground. The surrounding text explains soaking a shirt in sulfur water, drying it, and wearing it on the bare body for 5 to 6 days to cure itching without ointments.

Page 595 (recto) opens a new section on smallpox/pustules ('Von den Fladern der Kinder und alten Leüten'), featuring a watercolor portrait of a person with facial pustules, followed by etiology, prognosis, bloodletting/laxative advice, and internal remedies (antimony, bezoar, hartshorn, pulvis ruber pannonicus).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Person Afflicted with Facial Pustules (Smallpox / Fladern)** (`#xywh=400,400,2358,1000`): Watercolor illustration at the top of the left column depicting the head and shoulders of a person wearing a blue cap or headscarf and a pinkish-red pleated garment, with reddish-brown spots and pustules covering the face, framed at the bottom by a horizontal bar above the rubricated heading.

## Page 596 — IIIF Canvas #541

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0541.JP2` (5626 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0541.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0541.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of smallpox (Fladen) remedies: internal diaphoretics (carduus benedictus syrup, fumitory/scabious waters, Alkermes lozenges), remedies for suppressed eruption (hartshorn, bezoar/castoreum in broth), cooling gargles for smallpox in the mouth and throat, clearing blocked nostrils with butter and Marian water, and eye washes/ointments for swollen eyelids and eyes.

## Page 597 — IIIF Canvas #542

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0542.JP2` (6117 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0542.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0542.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen · **Tokens (In / Out):** 1,850 / 2,200

Continuation of smallpox (Fladen) care: protecting the eyes, prognostic signs, preventing facial pitting with barley mucilage, bean meal, egg oil, and virgin's milk, and removing pustules. Followed by a new section on 'Mania. Unsinnigkeit Verliehrung des Verstandts' (madness/loss of reason), describing its causes, symptoms, emetics, bloodletting, and diet.

Continuation of Mania (purging, bloodletting, and a rubricated 'Secretum' using dog brain). Followed by a new section on 'Ascheren Und Bluett Aissen' (carbuncles and blood-boils / furuncles), classifying parotids, axillary buboes, carbuncles, and pestilential swellings, and a subsection on 'Achslen Geschwern' (axillary abscesses).

| 22 | besers als alt hönig vd rockes mell undere- | better than old honey and rye flour mixed |
| 23 | ander vermischt mit gebradnen Zwieffel saft. vd | together with roasted onion juice, and |

| 25 | geschwindisten Zeitiget. Wanß dan disse gewalt | quickly. When then this force |
| 26 | ihr krafft ziehet, vd wan das geschwer Zeitig ist. | draws its power, and when the abscess is ripe, |
| 27 | Vd selbes nit auf bricht oder mans mit ainer | and itself does not break open, or one with a |

| 29 | stehn. damid der aiter nicht Zu weit vmb | so that the pus does not [eat] too far around |
| 30 | sich fresse, wan es nun geöfnet. laß den | itself; when it is now opened, let the |
| 31 | aiter heraus. Vd apliciere ain wairch lang | pus out, and apply a soft/long tent |
